Section VI. Many spiritual and corporal benefits have been granted after the death of this holy Virgin through her intercession.

[Under the patronage of this now-deceased Saint] Meanwhile letters from this holy Apostolic See arrived in Lima, so that witnesses concerning the life and deeds of this Handmaid of God might be examined according to the formula prescribed by the Congregation of Rites; which, having been begun in the year of the Lord 1630, could not be completed because of the great number of witnesses, who numbered one hundred and eighty-three, until the month of May in the year 1632. Inspectors, appointed as usual to visit the relics of Rose, upon opening the wooden chest found the virgin's little body after fifteen years had passed since her death, her garments consumed, her bones intact and still covered here and there with flesh, which exhaled the fragrance of roses.

[230] The Almighty God wished to show by the greatest signs what a sublime degree of glory in heaven Rose possesses, [many tepid to fervor,] whose most celebrated and frequent conversions, which surpass the creation of heaven and earth, were the conversions of sinners, which, obtained from God by the prayers of this Virgin, were remarkable: For at the mere touch of the virgin's little body in the coffin, so many were struck by sudden stings of compunction that they immediately, with raised voices, detested their own crimes, bursting into a tearful confession of their sins, as the great crowd of onlookers heard, saw, and marveled. Mother Virgin Mary of Oliva recounted before the judges that, after her daughter's funeral, several devoted persons—even previously unknown religious sisters—had come to her, relieving her poverty with generous aid and professing that they paid tribute to the Virgin Rose, whose intercessions they knew had brought about the best change in their lives.

[231] Bartholomew Martinez, formerly the confessor of this Virgin, declared on oath in court, [and sinners to penance,] a certain man of a dissolute life, who for a very long series of years had been sacrilegiously frequenting the sacraments, that he had been commended to the prayers of the already buried Rose, and soon he felt a motion of divine grace, by which his heart, melting, he most bitterly made a general confession of his entire life, and so the fear of the Lord pervaded his conscience, that thereafter he hastened to wash away whatever failings, as if they were the gravest excesses. It is also known that two women of infamous and sinful lives, moved by the fragrant fame of Rose, happily cast off the yoke of impurity and firmly set their foot on the path of virtue. The Limeños and others throughout the entire Peruvian region, the ministers of the sacrament of penance, upon exchanging reports, were generally astonished at such a change in the people's morals since the day Rosa was received into her Spouse's paradise; for the women, in a contest, cast aside the luxury of their clothing and everywhere adopted a simpler and more modest attire. The bars of the regulars resounded on all sides with penitence from the bloody blows of the whips. And this miracle was so publicly known that it was attested to under oath in court, that since the time Peru was discovered by our people, no preacher had ever shone there, who, with so many and such great incentives, had urged those peoples to virtue, and had kindled such a universal spirit of penance and a fire of devotion, as Rose had with her appearances, contacts, and prayers.

[232] Francisca and Alexander de Coloma, the siblings, had as their maternal aunt Maria de Xuata, a wealthy woman, [and others were stirred to a remarkable change of heart.] But she was implacably hostile in spirit toward her kinsmen, however poor they might be, the first of whom supported at his own expense six cousins—all of them still in their early years. Both were compelled to depart, leaving their little ones in utter destitution. Franciscus was a priest: he implores the protection of the Roses: the next day his aunt summons him, whom she had refused to see for the entire span of eighteen years. He orders the bringing in of his unfortunate cousins, and, in the presence of an attorney and a notary, having revoked the previous ineffectual will and made a gift to Francis, he names the little cousins as heirs of the estate, and the debts owed to Rosa are duly settled.

Ludovica Barba had heard from her confessor that Rosa had prophesied she would take the religious habit of Saint Dominic, which she herself detested. He long belittled the Virgin's words; indeed, he spurned the fulfillment of this oracle. At last a heavenly inspiration for the religious life seized her. She went to the tomb of Rose, humbly commended her cause to her, and suddenly a burning desire for the sacred Dominican habit was implanted in her. She was enrolled among the Third Order Sisters, filled with divine consolations, grieving only that she had so long rejected such happiness with a stubborn heart.

Ludovica de Mendoza could neither believe nor even conceive that the praises of this Virgin's sanctity were true, nor did she want to. Struck suddenly by a sharp pang in her hands and feet, she felt her spirit shaken by darkness and dread. He clearly recognized that this stemmed from his own single act of unbelief; he humbly protests that he believes, and with all his heart confesses, that Rosa is truly holy. And suddenly the storm subsided, and he felt those dreadful paroxysms recede.

[233] [Moreover, numerous] Besides the aforementioned spiritual benefits, many other and significant miracles performed in the healing of bodies are related, by which the holiness and merits of this Virgin have been amply proven: for from the proceedings conducted by apostolic authority, and formally discussed by the Sacred Congregation of Rites, with the case being presented by our beloved son Decio Cardinal Azzolini, it has been established not only that there is a reputation for sanctity, and for the theological and cardinal virtues with their annexes in a heroic degree, but furthermore, by conclusive proofs duly and rightly admitted, the following miracles were approved.

Maria Sanchez, also known as de Jesu, a nine-year-old girl, who, when she was six, accidentally fell to the ground and was so injured in her nerves and bones that, with her legs contracted and their sensation lost, she completely lost the ability to stand or walk, and was immediately confined to a bed for three years, growing older, was growing weaker with advancing age. When all natural remedies had failed, her parents, with humble hearts, vowed to place her in the tomb of the aforementioned Virgin for a novena to obtain her health: And on the last day of the novena, while they were fulfilling their vow, the little girl immediately rose to her feet above the tomb, to the amazement of all, walking about so nimbly as if she had never suffered such an injury.

[234] [afflicted with various diseases,] an apoplexy that ended in paralysis had withered the arm of Isabella Duran, painfully depriving her of all use of it for about five months; which, as a useless weight, was carried on the shoulder, wrapped in the bandages of the Predensians, and caused nothing but constant pain. He applied it to Rose's little body, which was just about to be buried. She soon unfastened her bandages, crying out aloud that she had been cared for most scrupulously, and publicly and forcefully proclaimed her faith in the miracle with a most agile gesture and movement of her arm, as if she had never been ill.

A certain Ethiopian servant of Diego de Ayala, due to nerve traction and a tenacious fold, had had his right arm and hand refuse to function for many years. And so, to the fame of the late Rose, he entered the church of Saint Dominic with the rest of the crowd, commended himself to the Holy Virgin, immediately stretched out his healed arm, and, raising it high, displayed it to the surrounding multitude with loud cries. Those who knew the servant more intimately came closer, congratulating him and not deigning to tire his hand with kisses, now that it was open and restored by Rose's kindness.

[235] [Having implored his help,] Magdalena Chimisso, a noble Indian woman (being of the lineage of the Chincian rulers), had for many years been miserably afflicted from the navel down with bodily atrophy, dryness of the legs, and feet, and could only be carried back and forth in the arms of others. Having tried all remedies in vain, in that deplorable state of health she lifted her heart to heaven, devoutly commended herself to the said Virgin, and touched her wounded parts with particles of her vestments, and instantly recovered perfect health, to the amazement of all who were present, on his own feet he went immediately to the church of the Preachers, where he publicly gave thanks to the said Virgin. Maria de Vera was at death's door for eight days, suffering from dysentery, a malignant fever, stomach pain, and a lethal vomiting. After receiving the Church's sacraments, she commended herself to the intercession of the aforementioned Rose, whose image she embraced, and was immediately seen to be healed with a full recovery of her strength. Leaping from her bed, she at once adorned the altar, placed the image there, and gave thanks for her restored health.

[236] Therefore, with the sanctity of Rose shining forth throughout the world by these and many other signs, [they were miraculously restored to health,] by her meritorious deeds, our predecessor of blessed memory, Pope Clement the Ninth, granted that this Servant of God be everywhere called Blessed, and, after celebrating her beatification with solemn rite, he decreed and declared her the principal patroness of the city of Lima and of the entire kingdom of Peru by apostolic authority, and commanded that her feast be observed by all who live there under penalty, and that her name be inserted in the Roman Martyrologyc. We also, seeing that she is everywhere most devoutly honored with solemn applause by all peoples, have established her as patroness of all the provinces, kingdoms, islands, and mainland regions of the entire Americas, and of the Philippines, and of the Indies, we have extended this patronage to all the provinces, kingdoms, islands, and regions of the whole continent of America, and of the Philippines, and, having formed processes by apostolic authority concerning those matters which had arisen and their validity approved, and the pious affections of the people in their veneration and devotion growing more and more, and with new signs and miracles being added*, of which, after mature deliberation, four were admitted; two, namely, from the Suessan processd, and two others from the Panormitan processe.

First. In the person of John Zelilli, miraculously preserved from imminent death: [after a severe trial] for he had been afflicted for four months by a third-degree remittent fever, which had progressed into an extreme marasmus with a lung ulcer, a cough spitting putrid blood, and a similarly ulcerated throat, to which a putrid and malignant fever had also been added; whereupon the patient, having received the sacraments of the Church, was brought to the final agony of his fleeting life and lay motionless, the pulses of his arteries ceased, and by the unanimous voice of three physicians it was proclaimed that he was already in the throes of death: but having implored the powerful help of the blessed Rose, he took a little powder from her tomb, dissolved in water with a spoon, and immediately recovered his health and former strength in the most perfect manner. The second, in Candida Rossetta, wife of the Spaniard Aloysius il Secondo de Carvajal, standard-bearer, who, in a miserable stillbirth, had been subjected for three days to excessive efforts to expel the afterbirth along with the placenta, which then, from the cold, hardened like marble, black, and hideous to behold, and of a prodigious size, being heavy with a weight of three pounds. She endured this most excruciating torment outdoors for eight years, until a paper image of this blessed one (to whom Candida had entrusted herself) was applied to her belly, and the bedridden woman felt the womb return to her womb. Immediately she leapt from her bed, healthy and strong, and was seen dancing about the house with joy.

[238] Two other miracles likewise from the Palermo process; [Reported from authentic documents.] namely, one in the miraculous healing of Fr. Seraphim Puglisi of the Order of the Blessed Mary of Mount Carmel. This religious man, having been fatally consumed by a malignant fever and on the seventeenth day of his illness completely abandoned by doctors who constantly affirmed that he would expire at about the fifth or sixth hour of the following night; And now, having lost the power of speech and struggling with her dying breath, as soon as she earnestly implored the help of Blessed Rose, she saw her clearly, and at once, strong, unhurt, and vigorous, she emerged to the astonishment of all. Finally, the most recent case was that of Angela Gabaja, who, afflicted with a double, continuous, and malignant tertian fever, with intense headaches and stomach pain, and tormented for twenty-seven days by a copious emission of flatulence, was at last openly declared by the physician to be dying. And, after six hours of labor in her agony, at her mother's urging she invoked the aid of the Blessed. Anointed with oil from a lamp hanging before that altar, on her stomach, neck, and chest, she was immediately and on the spot restored to her former health with a complete recovery of her broken strength.

We have presented the Decree of Beatification and other diplomas, by which Pope Clement enrolled this virgin among the number of the Blessed and extended her cult, in the preliminary commentary.

In January of the year 1669 the entry of this blessed Virgin was inserted into the Roman Martyrology, as appears from no. 31. The preceding commentaries are clear.

Sessa is a bishopric city of Italy, situated in the Kingdom of Naples under the Archbishop of Capua, where this process was instituted.

And Palermo is the most famous and principal city of Sicily, where the last two miracles were put to the test.

Marasmus, Greek marasmós, from the verb marainōmai, which means to waste away, is, in medicine, a type of wasting away that occurs from a fever so intense and widespread that it dries up the moisture of the entire body. If you wish, consult Castell in the medical lexicon, where on page 314 and following he describes this disease and its various species in greater detail.

Section Seven. The method and procedure of canonization, and the signatures of the most eminent members of the Sacred College. And of the Cardinals

[Pope Clement X indicates that,] Since therefore nothing was now lacking of those things which were necessary for the most sacred function of the canonization of this beloved Spouse of Christ, Rose, by the authority of the holy Fathers, the decrees of the sacred canons, and the ancient custom of the holy Roman Church, and by the prescription of the new decrees, at the request of our most beloved sons in Christ, the regent Queen Marianaa, and also of Charles IIb likewise King of Spain, the entire Order of Preachers of Saint Dominic, the Kingdom of Peru and all the provinces of America, and with the prayers of our venerable brothers the archbishops, and the bishops of the kingdoms of Spain and the Indies, we judged it just and fitting that the aforesaid Blessed, whom the Lord Himself daily more and more glorifies from heaven, should also be praised and glorified by us on earth with the office of veneration.

[240] Therefore, we have granted that on this day, publicly in the most sacred basilica of the Prince of the Apostles, [why he included the Rose of Lima among the Saints,] After the sacred Mass was concluded, her canonization was to be held, and on the same day in the aforementioned most holy Vatican Basilica, in which, with solemn rite, together with the Cardinals of the same Holy Roman Church, patriarchs, archbishops, and bishops, and our beloved sons, the prelates, officials, and household of the Roman Curia, the secular and regular clergy, and the greatest crowd of the people having assembled in the morning, the decree of canonization having been thrice repeated to us by the supplications of our beloved son Louis, Cardinal Portocarrero of the title of Saint Sabina, after sacred hymns, litanies, and other prayers duly implored for the grace of the Holy Spirit, to the honor of the holy and indivisible Trinity, and for the exaltation of the Catholic faith, by the authority of Almighty God, the Father, of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, of the blessed Apostles and of Ourselves, with the counsel of our venerable brothers, the Cardinals of the Holy Roman Church, the patriarchs, archbishops, and bishops in the Roman Curia, and with the unanimous consent, we declare and define the blessed Rose of Lima, virgin, whose holiness of life, sincerity of faith, and whose excellence of miracles is fully attested, we have defined to be a saint, and have decreed that she be enrolled in the catalog of holy Virgins, as we by the tenor of these presents decree, define, and enroll, establishing that her memory be celebrated by the universal Church among the holy virgins on the thirtieth day of the month of August each year. In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.

[241] By the same authority, to all and each who are truly penitent and have confessed, [and granted indulgences,] to all and each of the truly penitent and confessed, who in each year on the feast day of the same Saint Rose shall have approached to visit the sepulchre in which her body rests, we have mercifully in the Lord, in the usual form of the Church, remitted seven years and as many forty days of the penances enjoined upon them, or otherwise in any way due. These things being accomplished, we have venerated God the eternal Father, and Christ the Lord, the King of glory, the eternal Son of the Father, and the Holy Paraclete Spirit, one God and one Lord, with praises and confessions, and having solemnly sung the sacred hymn Te Deum, We prayed a special prayer in honor of Saint Rose through her merits, and at the altar of Saint Peter the Apostle we celebrated Mass with the commemoration of the same Saint, granting to all the faithful of Christ then present a plenary indulgence and remission of all their sins.

[242] With all humility, therefore, we bless and glorify God the almighty Father for such an outstanding benefit, [Gives thanks to God for that example of virtue,] to whom be blessing, and honor, and glory, and power, for ever and ever: earnestly praying to him, that through the intercession of his Electa, turning away his face from our sins, he may have mercy on us, and look upon us, to whom he has shown his mercy in the midst of his temple, while he has given us a new bulwark for all of America against the ancient snares of the devil and the errors of infidelity, and has granted to all a protective and advocate with his divine Majesty for the tranquility of the holy Church, the increase of the Catholic faith, and the light and conversion of infidels.

[243] [and he orders his decree to be promulgated everywhere,] However, since it would be difficult to have our present letters carried to every place where they might be needed, we decree that printed copies of them, subscribed by the hand of a public notary and sealed with the seal of any person in ecclesiastical office, shall have the same faith everywhere as the originals would have if they were exhibited or shown. Therefore, let no one whatsoever be permitted to infringe upon this page of our decree, definition, inscription, mandate, statute, concession, relaxation, grant, and will, or to act rashly against it. If anyone, however, presumes to attempt this, let him know that he will incur the indignation of Almighty God and of His blessed apostles Peter and Paul. Given at Rome at St. Peter's on the 13th day before the Ides of April, in the first year of our Pontificate, in the Lord's year 1671.

Notes.

Maria Anna of Austria, who was then administering the kingdom of Spain for Charles II, died in Madrid in 1696.

Charles II, King of Spain, succeeded Philip IV, and in the year 1700 he passed away, as is well known.

Although we recite the proper Office of St. Rose annually on August 30, we have nevertheless illustrated her Acts today, because on the 26th of this month that Saint is commemorated in the Roman Martyrology, as we noted in the preliminary commentary.
